Avoiding things? No, I don't think I've really been avoiding anything specific. Just doing my usual routine, you know? Going to the site, coming home. The site's been a mess lately, though. And the worst part is, the inspector is supposed to come tomorrow, and he's this real stickler for details. Last time he was here, he made us tear down a whole section of drywall because the screws were spaced like half an inch too far apart. He carries this little laser measuring tool on his belt like a six-shooter...

Oh, man, I'm doing it again, aren't I? Avoiding things? Yeah, no, I haven't been avoiding anything. Just haven't had much energy to do anything extra, but I'm not running away from anything.

My buddy Steve tried a plate once and he was sweating so bad his safety glasses kept sliding off his face the next day at work. He rides this massive chopper-style motorcycle to work, parks it right on the sidewalk out front. It's so loud it rattles the front window when he starts it up. If I don't enjoy things anymore? No, I don't think it's that. If I actually got out and did something, I'm sure it'd be fine. I just haven't really done anything fun lately to even find out, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're detached from yourself or your surroundings?\n\nPatient: Detached? Like, floating outside my body or something? No, I don't think I've ever felt anything weird like that. I'm definitely in my body, feeling every bit of how tired I am. My buddy Dave though, man, he was telling me about this time he had his wisdom teeth out and they gave him that laughing gas. He said he felt like he was floating up near the ceiling tiles looking down at the dentist. Which is crazy to think about, because Dave's a big guy, like six-two, two hundred and fifty pounds. Seeing him float would be like watching a refrigerator hover. He actually got his truck stuck in the mud out by the new subdivision site last week. Just sank it right up to the axles. We had to get the big CAT excavator to pull him out, and the operator, this guy named Mike, was just laughing his head off the whole time. Mike's missing half his front tooth from when he got hit in the face with a softball at the company picnic last year. He refused to go to the dentist to get it fixed because he says it makes him look like a pirate... \n\nWait, what were you asking me? Detached? Yeah, no, nothing like that for me. I'm just stressed and tired, not, like, out of my mind or anything.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding social situations or interactions with others because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: Avoiding people? I mean, I guess I haven't really been hanging out with anyone, but it's not like I'm hiding from them on purpose. I'm just too wiped out to deal with it. Like, the guys wanted to go get beers after we finished pouring the foundation at the new strip mall on Tuesday. We poured, man, it must have been like forty yards of concrete. The pump truck broke down halfway through, too. The hose got clogged or something, and the driver, this older guy named Hector, he was beating on the side of the pipe with a sledgehammer trying to knock it loose. Concrete was starting to set up in the hopper, it was a total mess. Hector was cussing up a storm in Spanish. He always wears these bright yellow suspenders over his high-vis shirt, looks like a giant bumblebee. Anyway, he finally got it cleared, but it put us behind like two hours, so by the time we finished floating the slab, it was getting dark and my back was absolutely killing me. I just wanted to go home and crash. \n\nWait, sorry, what did you ask? Avoiding people? Yeah, I'm not avoiding anyone, I just haven't been going out because I'm just so tired all the time from work and dealing with everything else. I just want to sleep when I get home.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in the things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Lost interest? I don't know, maybe? I guess I haven't really been doing much of anything lately besides working. Like I was saying about that concrete job, it just takes everything out of you. And my boots have been killing me, too. I bought these new Red Wings, spent like two hundred bucks on 'em, and they are taking forever to break in. The guy at the store, he had this crazy lazy eye, kept looking at my shoulder when he was talking to me. He swore up and down they'd be comfortable right out of the box. Total lie. I've got a blister on my heel the size of a quarter. I had to wrap it in duct tape yesterday just to make it through the shift. Duct tape fixes everything, right? My dad used to fix the muffler on his old Chevy with it. That truck was a piece of junk, rusted out floorboards, you could see the road going by under your feet... \n\nSorry, I'm rambling again. What did you ask? Lost interest? I don't think I've lost interest, I just... I haven't been doing my normal stuff. I'm just too exhausted to care about doing anything fun right now.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble concentrating on your work or daily tasks?\n\nPatient: Trouble concentrating? Yeah, I guess so. I mean, it's definitely been messing with my work lately. My foreman actually pulled me aside the other day and told me I could lose my shot at moving up to crew lead if I keep slipping up. Which really sucks because I've been busting my ass for that spot. The guy who has it now, Jimmy, he's retiring next month. Jimmy's this old school guy, smokes like two packs of Pall Malls a day, coughs like a dying tractor. He actually brought his pet ferret to the job site once. Had it on a little leash and everything. Tied it to the bumper of his truck while we were framing up a house. The framing inspector showed up, this real tight-ass guy with a clipboard, and the ferret got loose and ran right up his leg. I thought the inspector was gonna have a heart attack right there in the dirt. He started doing this weird little dance, kicking his leg around... \n\nWait, sorry, what was I saying? Oh, right, concentrating. Yeah, I've just been messing up. Like, I've had problems with the work paperwork and scheduling stuff lately because of all this. My head's just not in the game, you know?\n\nDoctor: Are there things you used to enjoy that don't give you any pleasure when you do them now?\n\nPatient: Things I used to enjoy? I mean... I don't know if it's that I don't get pleasure from them, I just haven't tried. Like, I used to go fishing down at the reservoir with my buddy Dave almost every weekend. We'd take his flat-bottom boat out, the one with the camo paint job that's peeling off on the sides. Dave's a character, man. He actually hooked himself in the earlobe once trying to cast a topwater frog under some willow branches. It was a treble hook, too, just buried right in there. We had to cut the barb off with a pair of rusty wire cutters we found in the bottom of his tackle box. The whole time, Dave's eating a gas station hot dog with his other hand, not even flinching. Said the hot dog was the only thing keeping him from passing out. It was one of those jalapeno cheddar ones, the kind that sit on those little rollers all day until they look like beef jerky...\n\nAnyway, what was the question? Not enjoying stuff? Yeah, I don't know. Like I said before, I just haven't been doing my normal stuff. I haven't even thought about going fishing or anything else. I'm just too wiped out to try.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Weight? No, I don't think so. I haven't really noticed any change. I mean, my jeans still fit the same. Though, speaking of jeans, I ripped my favorite pair last week on a piece of rebar sticking out of a foundation wall. Just caught it right on the thigh and tore a massive hole. They were those Carhartt ones, the double-front kind, too. You know how tough those are supposed to be? I was so mad. I had to go to the surplus store on my lunch break to buy a new pair because I couldn't exactly walk around the site with half my leg hanging out. The guy at the surplus store was wearing this full tactical vest behind the counter, like he was expecting a raid or something. He kept trying to sell me on these military MREs, telling me they were better than whatever I brought for lunch. He had a whole shelf of them, said the beef stew one was his favorite... \n\nSorry, I'm doing it again. What did you ask me? Weight loss? Yeah, no, I haven't lost any weight. Just regular, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to experience a full range of emotions, like happiness or sadness, when something good or bad happens to you?\n\nPatient: Emotions? Yeah, I mean, I think so. I haven't really noticed feeling numb or anything like that. I guess I just feel... normal? Well, normal for right now, anyway. Speaking of normal, we had this guy start on the crew last week, total greenhorn. Didn't even know how to swing a hammer without bending the nail. We told him to go to the truck and get a board stretcher, and the kid actually spent twenty minutes looking for it. He even asked the drywall guys if they had one he could borrow. The drywall crew, they're a whole different breed, man. They listen to this crazy loud polka music all day, just blasting it from a boombox covered in plaster dust. One of them, this huge guy named Boris, he eats an entire raw onion like an apple with his lunch. Just bites right into it, crunch, crunch, crunch. He says it's good for his immune system, but man, you can smell him from three rooms away...\n\nWait, sorry. Emotions, right? Happiness and sadness? Yeah, I don't think I've lost the ability to feel them or anything. I just haven't had a lot of reasons to feel happy lately, you know? But I can still feel stuff.\n\nDoctor: Are there specific things or situations that you've been avoiding because of how they make you feel?\n\nPatient: Avoiding things? No, I don't think I've really been avoiding anything specific. Just doing my usual routine, you know? Going to the site, coming home. The site's been a mess lately, though. The general contractor ordered the wrong size windows for the whole second floor. Can you believe that? Thirty-two windows, all of them two inches too wide for the rough openings. The framing crew was so ticked off. The foreman, this guy named Mike who smokes those little cigars that smell like vanilla, he just stood there staring at the pallets of windows for like ten minutes, completely silent. Then he just turned around, walked to his truck, and drove off. Didn't come back until after lunch. We had to spend the rest of the day re-framing all the openings. It was a nightmare. And the worst part is, the inspector is supposed to come tomorrow, and he's this real stickler for details. Last time he was here, he made us tear down a whole section of drywall because the screws were spaced like half an inch too far apart. He carries this little laser measuring tool on his belt like a six-shooter...\n\nOh, man, I'm doing it again, aren't I? Avoiding things? Yeah, no, I haven't been avoiding anything. Just haven't had much energy to do anything extra, but I'm not running away from anything.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Frightening or upsetting? Like, recently? No, not really. Nothing crazy like that. I mean, the job site is always a little sketchy, but nothing out of the ordinary. Well, actually, there was this one time last week when the crane operator almost dropped a pallet of roofing shingles on the boss's truck. The wind caught it weird, and the whole load just started swinging like a pendulum. You should have seen the boss run. He's this short, stocky guy who always wears these bright yellow suspenders, and he was sprinting across the mud like he was in the Olympics. He dove behind a pile of cinder blocks just as the pallet swung over his truck. It didn't hit it, but it was close. The crane operator, this old guy named Earl who's been doing it since the eighties, he just got on the radio and said, 'Oops.' Just 'Oops.' He's always so calm. He brings a thermos of black coffee every day, and I swear he drinks it boiling hot. He says it keeps his hands steady. I don't know how he does it, my stomach would be in knots...

Wait, what was the question? Frightening stuff? No, no, I haven't had anything like that happen to me. Just the usual day-to-day stuff, you know? Nothing extreme.

Recurring memories? Intrusive thoughts? No, I haven't really had anything like that since I moved into the new room. It's just a room, you know? Just four walls and a bed. Excessive worry? Hard to control? Nah, not really. I mean, I think about work and stuff, but it's not out of control. Just normal stuff. Speaking of normal, the new guy we hired last week, Jimmy, he is anything but. He showed up on his first day wearing these pristine, bright white work boots. Like, not a scuff on them. Who wears white boots to a mud pit? The boss took one look at him and just shook his head. Anyway, Jimmy is terrified of heights, which is a problem because we're framing a two-story house right now. Yesterday, we needed him to go up on the scaffolding to hand up some two-by-fours, and the guy just froze. He was hugging the ladder like it was his long-lost mother. He wouldn't go up, wouldn't come down. Just stuck there, halfway up, eyes squeezed shut. The drywall guys, who are always blasting this super loud polka music from a boombox that looks like it survived a war, they started taking bets on how long he'd stay up there. One guy, Hector, he even offered Jimmy a taco if he'd just move an inch. Jimmy eventually had to be coaxed down by Earl, the crane operator, who just stood at the bottom holding his thermos of boiling coffee and telling him a story about a time he got stuck on a Ferris wheel in 1992... 

Oh, right, sorry. Worrying. Yeah, no, I'm not excessively worried about anything. Work is work, the room is the room. It's all fine.

The general contractor ordered the wrong size windows for the whole second floor. Can you believe that? Thirty-two windows, all of them two inches too wide for the rough openings. The framing crew was so ticked off. The foreman, this guy named Mike who smokes those little cigars that smell like vanilla, he just stood there staring at the pallets of windows for like ten minutes, completely silent. Then he just turned around, walked to his truck, and drove off. Didn't come back until after lunch. We had to spend the rest of the day re-framing all the openings. It was a nightmare. And the worst part is, the inspector is supposed to come tomorrow, and he's this real stickler for details. Last time he was here, he made us tear down a whole section of drywall because the screws were spaced like half an inch too far apart. He carries this little laser measuring tool on his belt like a six-shooter...\n\nOh, man, I'm doing it again, aren't I? Avoiding things? Yeah, no, I haven't been avoiding anything. Just haven't had much energy to do anything extra, but I'm not running away from anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced anything recently that was extremely frightening or upsetting to you?\n\nPatient: Frightening or upsetting? Like, recently? No, not really. Nothing crazy like that. I mean, the job site is always a little sketchy, but nothing out of the ordinary. Well, actually, there was this one time last week when the crane operator almost dropped a pallet of roofing shingles on the boss's truck. The wind caught it weird, and the whole load just started swinging like a pendulum. You should have seen the boss run. He's this short, stocky guy who always wears these bright yellow suspenders, and he was sprinting across the mud like he was in the Olympics. He dove behind a pile of cinder blocks just as the pallet swung over his truck. It didn't hit it, but it was close. The crane operator, this old guy named Earl who's been doing it since the eighties, he just got on the radio and said, 'Oops.' Just 'Oops.' He's always so calm. He brings a thermos of black coffee every day, and I swear he drinks it boiling hot. He says it keeps his hands steady. I don't know how he does it, my stomach would be in knots...\n\nWait, what was the question? Frightening stuff? No, no, I haven't had anything like that happen to me. Just the usual day-to-day stuff, you know? Nothing extreme.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant losses, like the death of a loved one or the end of a relationship, recently?\n\nPatient: Losses? Like someone dying or a breakup? No, no, nothing like that. Everyone's healthy, thank God. Speaking of healthy, my buddy Sal on the crew, he decided he's going on this crazy juice cleanse thing. Says he watched some documentary on the internet and now he's convinced solid food is poisoning us. The guy works construction, right? Swinging a sledgehammer all day, and all he brings for lunch is this giant thermos of green sludge. It looks like pond water. Smells like grass clippings and despair. Yesterday, we were pouring concrete for this new foundation, and Sal is just sweating buckets, looking paler than the cement mix. The concrete truck driver, this lady named Brenda who's got a pet iguana she keeps in the cab with her—she actually puts a little harness on it and lets it sunbathe on the dashboard—Brenda looks at Sal and tells him he's going to pass out right into the forms and become a permanent part of the patio. Sal just takes a swig of his green sludge and tries to flex, but he ended up having to sit in the shade for an hour eating saltines the boss had in his glovebox... \n\nOh, right, sorry. What were we talking about? Oh, losses. Yeah, no, no deaths or breakups or anything like that. Just the usual grind.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any recurring, intrusive memories or thoughts about anything in particular since you moved into your new room?\n\nPatient: Recurring memories? Intrusive thoughts? No, I haven't really had anything like that since I moved into the new room. It's just a room, you know? Just four walls and a bed. Speaking of beds, I had to buy a new mattress when I moved in, and let me tell you, mattress shopping is a scam. I went to this place out on Route 9, and the salesman, he was wearing this suit that looked like it was made out of upholstery fabric, like a couch from the seventies. He kept trying to sell me this 'space-age memory foam' thing that cost more than my truck. He made me lie down on it right there in the showroom, and then he started bouncing on the bed next to me to show me how it doesn't transfer motion. He had this little glass of water he set on my mattress, and he's jumping up and down, and the water isn't spilling. I'm just lying there under the fluorescent lights, trying not to make eye contact with the other customers. I ended up just buying a regular spring mattress from a guy off Craigslist. He had it strapped to the roof of a Honda Civic with bungee cords, and we had to carry it up three flights of stairs...\n\nWait, sorry, what was the question? Intrusive thoughts? Yeah, no, nothing like that. My mind's pretty quiet when I'm in the room. Just trying to get some sleep, mostly.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing excessive worry about your living situation or work that's been hard to control?\n\nPatient: Excessive worry? Hard to control? Nah, not really. I mean, I think about work and stuff, but it's not out of control. Just normal stuff. Speaking of normal, the new guy we hired last week, Jimmy, he is anything but. He showed up on his first day wearing these pristine, bright white work boots. Like, not a scuff on them. Who wears white boots to a mud pit? The boss took one look at him and just shook his head. Anyway, Jimmy is terrified of heights, which is a problem because we're framing a two-story house right now. Yesterday, we needed him to go up on the scaffolding to hand up some two-by-fours, and the guy just froze. He was hugging the ladder like it was his long-lost mother. He wouldn't go up, wouldn't come down. Just stuck there, halfway up, eyes squeezed shut. The drywall guys, who are always blasting this super loud polka music from a boombox that looks like it survived a war, they started taking bets on how long he'd stay up there. One guy, Hector, he even offered Jimmy a taco if he'd just move an inch. Jimmy eventually had to be coaxed down by Earl, the crane operator, who just stood at the bottom holding his thermos of boiling coffee and telling him a story about a time he got stuck on a Ferris wheel in 1992... \n\nOh, right, sorry. Worrying. Yeah, no, I'm not excessively worried about anything. Work is work, the room is the room. It's all fine."
  }
